Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me, I really appreciate you taking the time. 

Um... I guess the main thing that finally pushed me to come in is... well, I've been having a little trouble at work. Um... I wouldn't say I feel anxious, exactly. I definitely don't want to make it sound like a severe issue or anything. But maybe... maybe just a little restless?

I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or restless when you're sitting at your desk before you get up to leave?\n\nPatient: Um... I wouldn't say I feel anxious, exactly. I definitely don't want to make it sound like a severe issue or anything. I end up shifting around in my chair quite a bit, too, even though I'm really trying my absolute best to look focused and be respectful to whoever is speaking. Um... well... I guess if I really think about it, some of the... the trouble with keeping track of things isn't entirely new? I mean, I wasn't really a hyper kid or anything, I didn't run around the classroom! I was a good kid, I promise.

Um, no, definitely not. I would never want to be rude like that. I was always really careful to raise my hand and wait to be called on. 

I mean, I'm sure maybe once or twice I got excited about something and spoke out of turn, but... but that was rare. I always tried to be very respectful of the teachers and the other students. I hated the idea of talking over someone or making them feel like I wasn't listening. 

Even now, I'm usually the one waiting for a pause in the conversation to speak up. I just... I think it's important to be polite and let people finish their thoughts, you know? I really hope I'm coming across that way. I try very hard to be considerate of others.

I was doing really well before that. I just... I guess the new structure has been a bit of an adjustment for me. Does that timeline help? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right context for all of this.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any habits or rituals that you feel compelled to do in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Um... no, no, nothing like that. I don't have any rituals or anything I feel like I *have* to do in a certain way. I'm pretty flexible, honestly! I always try to just go with the flow and do things however they need to be done, especially at work. \n\nI definitely don't have any habits that I'm stuck on. I just... I try to be adaptable, you know? I hope that's what you were asking. I'm not, like, rigid or anything like that, I promise. Is that a good answer?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I really try to be careful about what I do. I don't want to make any mistakes or, you know, cause problems for anyone, especially at work with the claims. So I always try to think things through before I make a decision or do something. \n\nI'm really not the type to just jump into things recklessly, I promise. I always want to make sure I'm doing things the right way and being responsible. Does that... does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Did you have any of these behaviors, such as fidgeting or getting up from your desk, when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I was a good kid, I promise. \n\nBut, um... in elementary school, my teachers would sometimes write on my report cards that I was, you know, bright and capable. They just... they also mentioned that I would sometimes lose my worksheets or miss part of the instructions. But I always tried my best to be a good student. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a problem child or anything... is that the kind of thing you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Did you experience restlessness or have trouble sitting still as a child?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I really don't think so. I was actually a very quiet kid. I didn't bounce off the walls or, you know, disrupt the class or anything like that. \n\nI was perfectly happy just sitting at my desk. Like I mentioned before, maybe I was daydreaming a little bit sometimes, but I definitely wasn't restless. I always wanted to make sure I wasn't a bother to my teachers, so I made sure to sit still and be polite. I hope that's a good thing? I just... I really tried hard to be well-behaved and follow the rules. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Did you often have trouble paying attention or following instructions when you were in school?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I guess I did have a little bit of trouble sometimes? But really, I wasn't a bad student, I promise. I always tried my hardest to listen to the teachers and do exactly what they asked. \n\nIt's just that sometimes, you know, if the teacher was talking for a really long time, my mind would sort of... drift off a little. And then I might miss a step in the directions. But I always felt terrible when that happened! I'd usually just try to figure it out quietly by looking at what the other kids were doing, so I wouldn't have to interrupt the teacher or be a nuisance. \n\nI really wanted to do well and make my parents and teachers happy. I mean, everyone has a little trouble paying attention sometimes, right? I hope that doesn't make it sound like I was ignoring them on purpose... does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced difficulties with sitting still or fidgeting consistently over the years, or has it varied?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really.
